For throw, the pre-cleanup state is checkpointed before `finally` and fields
dirtied by cleanup are checkpointed again immediately before the activation
disappears. Each propagated method does the same; no dynamic imported value is
copied back and no suspended caller is reloaded. Any other control edge which
destroys a generated C activation, including a failing `assert`, `stop` or
cancellation, passes through that final epilogue as well.

`retry` is a local control-flow transfer, not a call. It performs no additional
checkpoint solely because the retry edge is taken, preserves the current C
working activation and does not roll graph state back. Calls or exits reached
during the repeated execution establish their normal boundaries.

`yield` suspends the ordinary activation and hands control/a value outside that
activation, so it performs a dirty-only checkpoint before suspension. A stackful backend retains the C frame;
a stackless backend may preserve an equivalent private continuation frame.
Neither choice creates a source-visible Namespace or makes the method Structure
the activation history. Resumption restores the same C working values and dirty
state after that successful checkpoint, not a reload from the method node. This
semantic boundary is independent of whether the backend implements suspension
as a C call, a stack switch or a state-machine return. The producer's hidden
dynamic values remain those retained from its ordinary initial C invocation; a
subsequent `next` wrapper's caller visibility does not replace them. If the
suspension crosses an end-turn collection boundary, every retained Lmx reference
in the continuation is a registered collector root until resume/close.

21.11. Repeated Names and Structural Updates

Repeated field names are ordered child occurrences, not stacked dynamic
bindings:

    field == [0]field
    [1]field is the next matching child in forward result order

A later declaration or merge operand never silently replaces the unqualified
default. Dynamic caller precedence from 21.3 selects an activation value; it
does not reorder children or modify this occurrence rule.

An explicit operation may append, replace, remove or move occurrences only when
its receiver defines that graph mutation. Every affected named node must keep
the `Lmx * -> ShortNameId` reverse index coherent.

21.12. Compiler Work Tables and Replacement-Core Boundary

The parser and translator may use source symbol tables, call-graph/fixed-point
tables, ShortNameId interning, method records, signature tables and fixup lists.
They use that compiler namespace to determine each generated C formal and actual
argument. These are translation data, not a runtime Namespace object.

Across modules, a Structure dependency resolves to an Lmx pointer, an explicit
root-relative lookup or a provider returning an Lmx pointer. A method dependency
resolves to an admitted `all_methods_array` record. Compilation/linking must
make every callable entry which may appear in the program graph reachable; it
must not depend on a top-level-only traversal which omits nested method bodies.

The replacement translator core uses generic Lmx construction, traversal and
runtime merge, typed method records, generated C activation values and the
dirty-only checkpoint protocol. Direct links, cached child positions, static
dirty proofs, inlining and recursion-to-loop lowering are permitted
optimizations only when they preserve this observable model.

The current hosted implementation's descriptor gates, per-name typedefs,
top-level-only executable traversal, incomplete Consumer-used-tree checking and
old uniform one-pointer calls are implementation debt. They are not normative
intermediate stages of Revision 2.

21.13. Remaining Call-Boundary and Publication Choices

The following details remain deliberately unresolved. They do not reopen the
fixed dynamic-caller, reserved-`node`, typed-call, dirty-only, no-reload or
Message-serialization semantics of sections 21.1--21.12:

1. The explicit source spelling for navigating above the immediate containing
   Structure denoted by reserved `node`. Whatever spelling is selected must not
   add an implicit lexical-parent search or an extra parent hop to
   `node\field`.
2. The publication conflict policy when nested code structurally removes,
   moves or replaces the exact own-field occurrence retained by a suspended
   activation. A bootstrap may reject that mutation. No implementation may
   silently select a new `[0]` occurrence or reinsert the old one.
3. The checkpoint-store failure policy: infallible publication,
   prevalidated/all-or-nothing publication, or a specified deterministic
   prefix-commit diagnostic. In every profile, an outbound call or normal exit
   waits for all required successful publications, and the policy states the
   visible stores and dirty flags after failure without inventing an implicit
   rollback history.
4. The physical result, declared-throw and runtime-failure carrier in generated
   C. Its exact encoding is open; its source contract is part of the exact
   method `sig`, and every activation-destroying path still executes the
   required checkpoint/cleanup ordering.
5. The binary layout, relocation and adapter representation of
   `all_methods_array` records. The representation is open, while their
   complete versioned canonical signature identity and exact typed invocation
   are fixed.

21.H. Revision-1 Lexical Namespace Model (Historical)

Historical text and examples: `Lingvamyxa_spec_history.txt`, section 21.H.
